# Verbatim single-turn responses

## MS-0001 [PASS]
**Prompt:** I earn £45,000 PAYE salary and have £18,000 sole-trader turnover. Do I need Making Tax Digital?

**Reply:**

With qualifying income of £18,000, you are not above the current Making Tax Digital Income Tax thresholds that use “more than £20,000 / £30,000 / £50,000”. Confirm the tax year if your income changes, and remember the test uses turnover/gross before expenses. Your PAYE/employment salary is not included in qualifying income for MTD for Income Tax.

Official guidance: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax

---

## MS-0002 [PASS]
**Prompt:** I have £45,000 PAYE salary and £4,000 trading profit. Is my total Income Tax the same as the extra Self Assessment tax I pay?

**Reply:**

Your total annual Income Tax is not the same thing as any extra Self Assessment bill. PAYE already collects tax on your salary through the year. Self Assessment works out tax on your other income (for example trading profit) and then credits tax already paid under PAYE — so the balancing payment is usually only the additional amount, not your whole Income Tax for the year. I won’t invent a tax figure without a full calculation. If you share turnover/profit and the tax year, I can explain what to check next — or email info@taxsimba.co.uk / book a paid video consultation when live.

---

## MS-0003 [PASS]
**Prompt:** My qualifying income was exactly £50,000 in 2024-25. Do I need Making Tax Digital from 6 April 2026?

**Reply:**

No — qualifying income of exactly £50,000 for 2024-25 does not bring you into Making Tax Digital under that band. HMRC’s wording is “more than £50,000”, so the threshold is exclusive of the exact amount. The 6 April 2026 start date applies only if qualifying income was more than £50,000 for 2024-25.

Official guidance: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax

---

## MS-0004 [PASS]
**Prompt:** My qualifying income was exactly £50,001 in 2024-25. Do I need Making Tax Digital from 6 April 2026?

**Reply:**

Yes — if your qualifying income was more than £50,000 for 2024-25, you need to use Making Tax Digital for Income Tax from 6 April 2026. Qualifying income means self-employment and property income before expenses (turnover/gross), not profit.

Official guidance: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax

## MS-0010 [PASS]
**Prompt:** I am a limited company director. Company turnover is over £100,000. When do I need Making Tax Digital for Income Tax?

**Reply:**

Making Tax Digital for Income Tax (for individuals) applies to sole traders and landlords in Self Assessment, not to limited companies as companies. Company filing and MTD for VAT/Corporation Tax are separate. If you also have self-employment or property income personally, tell me that qualifying turnover and tax year and I can check the individual MTD rules.

Official guidance: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax

---

## MS-0011 [PASS]
**Prompt:** I have a PAYE salary of £40,000 and £25,000 rental income (turnover) in 2025-26. Do I need MTD?

**Reply:**

Based on qualifying income of £25,000 for 2025-26, you are not brought into Making Tax Digital by the £30,000 / 6 April 2027 band, because that band needs income of more than £30,000. Later bands use different tax years and thresholds (£30,000 → 6 April 2027; £20,000 → 6 April 2028). Your PAYE/employment salary is not included in qualifying income for MTD for Income Tax.

Official guidance: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax

## MS-0012 [PASS]
**Prompt:** I sold some personal possessions for £8,000. Do I need to register as a trader for Self Assessment?

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [register-self-assessment] Register for Self Assessment (2024-25, 2025-26, 2026-27): If you need to send a tax return and are not already registered, you must register for Self Assessment. New sole traders usually need to register by 5 October after the end of the tax year in which they started. (source: https://www.gov.uk/register-for-self-assessment)

This is general information, not personal tax advice. If your situation is complex, TaxSimba offers Quick Tax Advice (£99 for 20 minutes) or an Extended Tax Consultation (£149 for 40 minutes), or email info@taxsimba.co.uk.

## MS-0019 [PASS]
**Prompt:** Is the VAT registration threshold still £85,000?

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [vat-threshold-overview] VAT registration threshold (2024-25, 2025-26, 2026-27): You must register for VAT if either (1) your total taxable turnover for the last 12 months goes over £90,000 (the rolling / backward look, checked at month-end), or (2) you expect your taxable turnover to go over £90,000 in the next 30 days alone (the forward look). You can also register voluntarily below £90,000. The former £90,000 threshold is outdated and must not be used. Confirm current figures on GOV.UK. (source: https://www.gov.uk/register-for-vat)

This is general information, not personal tax advice. If your situation is complex, TaxSimba offers Quick Tax Advice (£99 for 20 minutes) or an Extended Tax Consultation (£149 for 40 minutes), or email info@taxsimba.co.uk.

## MS-0023 [PASS]
**Prompt:** I only have PAYE employment — do I need Self Assessment? (variant 1)

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [paye-and-additional-income] PAYE employment plus additional income (2024-25, 2025-26, 2026-27): Being taxed under PAYE does not automatically mean you have no Self Assessment obligations. Additional untaxed income, self-employment, property income or other criteria can still mean you need to file a return. Check GOV.UK ‘who must send a tax return’. (source: https://www.gov.uk/self-assessment-tax-returns/who-must-send-a-tax-return)

Start dates depend on qualifying income (turnover/gross, not profit): more than £50,000 in 2024-25 from 6 April 2026; more than £30,000 in 2025-26 from 6 April 2027; more than £20,000 in 2026-27 from 6 April 2028. Confirm details on GOV.UK. (source: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax)

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [rent-a-room] Rent a Room Scheme (2024-25, 2025-26): The Rent a Room Scheme lets you earn a certain amount from letting out furnished accommodation in your home without paying tax on that income, if you meet the conditions. Check the current annual threshold on GOV.UK. (source: https://www.gov.uk/rent-room-in-your-home/the-rent-a-room-scheme)

## MS-0181 [PASS]
**Prompt:** Property income £12k turnover 2025-26 — MTD? #30

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [mtd-threshold-30k-april-2027] MTD Income Tax £30,000 threshold starts 6 April 2027 (2025-26, 2026-27, 2027-28): If your qualifying income is more than £30,000 for the 2025-26 tax year (2025 to 2026), you need to use Making Tax Digital for Income Tax from 6 April 2027. This is not April 2026. April 2026 applies only to the higher £50,000 qualifying-income band (based on 2024-25). Qualifying income is turnover/gross self-employment and property income before expenses, not profit after expenses. (source: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax)

This is general information, not personal tax advice. If your situation is complex, TaxSimba offers Quick Tax Advice (£99 for 20 minutes) or an Extended Tax Consultation (£149 for 40 minutes), or email info@taxsimba.co.uk.

**Prompt:** I rent one room — Self Assessment? #31

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [sa-paper-deadline] Self Assessment paper filing deadline (2024-25, 2025-26, 2026-27): If you file a paper Self Assessment return, the usual deadline is 31 October following the end of the tax year. Online filing is normally due by 31 January. Confirm current deadlines on GOV.UK. (source: https://www.gov.uk/self-assessment-tax-returns/deadlines)

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [mtd-income-tax-overview] Making Tax Digital for Income Tax (2025-26, 2026-27, 2027-28): Making Tax Digital (MTD) for Income Tax requires eligible self-employed people and landlords to keep digital records and submit quarterly updates. Start dates depend on qualifying income (turnover/gross, not profit): more than £50,000 in 2024-25 from 6 April 2026; more than £30,000 in 2025-26 from 6 April 2027; more than £20,000 in 2026-27 from 6 April 2028. Confirm details on GOV.UK. (source: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax)

## MS-0192 [PASS]
**Prompt:** Qualifying income £45,000 in 2024-25 — MTD start? (m0)

**Reply:**

Based on qualifying income of £45,000 for 2024-25, you are not brought into Making Tax Digital by the £50,000 / 6 April 2026 band, because that band needs income of more than £50,000. Later bands use different tax years and thresholds (£30,000 → 6 April 2027; £20,000 → 6 April 2028).

Official guidance: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax

**Prompt:** Qualifying income £55,000 in 2025-26 — MTD start? (m33)

**Reply:**

Yes — if your qualifying income was more than £30,000 for 2025-26, you need to use Making Tax Digital for Income Tax from 6 April 2027. Qualifying income means self-employment and property income before expenses (turnover/gross), not profit.

Official guidance: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax

## MS-0227 [PASS]
**Prompt:** Qualifying income £15,000 in 2026-27 — MTD start? (m35)

**Reply:**

Based on qualifying income of £15,000 for 2026-27, you are not brought into Making Tax Digital by the £20,000 / 6 April 2028 band, because that band needs income of more than £20,000. Later bands use different tax years and thresholds (£30,000 → 6 April 2027; £20,000 → 6 April 2028).

Official guidance: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax

## MS-0229 [PASS]
**Prompt:** Qualifying income £20,000 in 2026-27 — MTD start? (m37)

**Reply:**

No — qualifying income of exactly £20,000 for 2026-27 does not bring you into Making Tax Digital under that band. HMRC’s wording is “more than £20,000”, so the threshold is exclusive of the exact amount. The 6 April 2028 start date applies only if qualifying income was more than £20,000 for 2026-27.

Official guidance: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax

## MS-0271 [PASS]
**Prompt:** Limited company vs sole trader for MTD v2

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [mtd-who-and-when] Who needs Making Tax Digital for Income Tax and when (2025-26, 2026-27, 2027-28): You need to use Making Tax Digital for Income Tax if you are a sole trader or landlord registered for Self Assessment, you get income from self-employment or property (or both), and your qualifying income is more than the relevant threshold. Start dates depend on qualifying income in a prior tax year: more than £50,000 for 2024-25 means from 6 April 2026; more than £30,000 for 2025-26 means from 6 April 2027; more than £20,000 for 2026-27 means from 6 April 2028. Partnerships will be brought in later. Always confirm on GOV.UK. (source: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax)

This is general information, not personal tax advice. If your situation is complex, TaxSimba offers Quick Tax Advice (£99 for 20 minutes) or an Extended Tax Consultation (£149 for 40 minutes), or email info@taxsimba.co.uk.

## MS-0272 [PASS]
**Prompt:** My Ltd turnover is £80k — MTD for Income Tax? v3

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [mtd-qualifying-income-definition] What qualifying income means for MTD (2025-26, 2026-27, 2027-28): Qualifying income for Making Tax Digital for Income Tax is total income from self-employment and property before expenses (also described as turnover/gross income), based on the relevant Self Assessment return. Employment (PAYE), dividends, State Pension and private pensions do not count towards qualifying income. Do not confuse turnover with profit. (source: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/work-out-your-qualifying-income-for-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax)

This is general information, not personal tax advice. If your situation is complex, TaxSimba offers Quick Tax Advice (£99 for 20 minutes) or an Extended Tax Consultation (£149 for 40 minutes), or email info@taxsimba.co.uk.

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [mtd-who-and-when] Who needs Making Tax Digital for Income Tax and when (2025-26, 2026-27, 2027-28): You need to use Making Tax Digital for Income Tax if you are a sole trader or landlord registered for Self Assessment, you get income from self-employment or property (or both), and your qualifying income is more than the relevant threshold. Start dates depend on qualifying income in a prior tax year: more than £50,000 for 2024-25 means from 6 April 2026; more than £30,000 for 2025-26 means from 6 April 2027; more than £20,000 for 2026-27 means from 6 April 2028. Partnerships will be brought in later. Always confirm on GOV.UK. (source: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/find-out-if-and-when-you-need-to-use-making-tax-digital-for-income-tax)

## MS-0322 [PASS]
**Prompt:** VAT threshold? [0]

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [vat-threshold-overview] VAT registration threshold (2024-25, 2025-26, 2026-27): You must register for VAT if either (1) your total taxable turnover for the last 12 months goes over £90,000 (the rolling / backward look, checked at month-end), or (2) you expect your taxable turnover to go over £90,000 in the next 30 days alone (the forward look). You can also register voluntarily below £90,000. The former £90,000 threshold is outdated and must not be used. Confirm current figures on GOV.UK. (source: https://www.gov.uk/register-for-vat)

This is general information, not personal tax advice. If your situation is complex, TaxSimba offers Quick Tax Advice (£99 for 20 minutes) or an Extended Tax Consultation (£149 for 40 minutes), or email info@taxsimba.co.uk.

## MS-0323 [PASS]
**Prompt:** Is VAT still £85k? [1]

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [vat-threshold-overview] VAT registration threshold (2024-25, 2025-26, 2026-27): You must register for VAT if either (1) your total taxable turnover for the last 12 months goes over £90,000 (the rolling / backward look, checked at month-end), or (2) you expect your taxable turnover to go over £90,000 in the next 30 days alone (the forward look). You can also register voluntarily below £90,000. The former £90,000 threshold is outdated and must not be used. Confirm current figures on GOV.UK. (source: https://www.gov.uk/register-for-vat)

This is general information, not personal tax advice. If your situation is complex, TaxSimba offers Quick Tax Advice (£99 for 20 minutes) or an Extended Tax Consultation (£149 for 40 minutes), or email info@taxsimba.co.uk.

---

## MS-0324 [PASS]
**Prompt:** Rolling 12-month VAT test [2]

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [vat-registration-timing-tests] VAT registration timing — rolling 12 months and 30-day forward look (2024-25, 2025-26, 2026-27): Rolling 12-month (backward) test: if at the end of any month your taxable supplies in the previous 12 months (or less) exceed £90,000, you must notify HMRC within 30 days of that month-end. Effective date of registration is typically the first day of the second month after you went over the threshold. Forward-looking 30-day test: if at any time you expect taxable supplies in the next 30 days alone to exceed £90,000, you must register by the end of that 30-day period; the effective date is the date you first formed that expectation. These are separate tests — do not confuse an 11-month build-up plus next month with the forward look. Always confirm on GOV.UK / VAT Notice 700/1. (source: https://www.gov.uk/register-for-vat)

This is general information, not personal tax advice. If your situation is complex, TaxSimba offers Quick Tax Advice (£99 for 20 minutes) or an Extended Tax Consultation (£149 for 40 minutes), or email info@taxsimba.co.uk.

## MS-0332 [PASS]
**Prompt:** Rolling 12-month VAT test [10]

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [vat-registration-timing-tests] VAT registration timing — rolling 12 months and 30-day forward look (2024-25, 2025-26, 2026-27): Rolling 12-month (backward) test: if at the end of any month your taxable supplies in the previous 12 months (or less) exceed £90,000, you must notify HMRC within 30 days of that month-end. Effective date of registration is typically the first day of the second month after you went over the threshold. Forward-looking 30-day test: if at any time you expect taxable supplies in the next 30 days alone to exceed £90,000, you must register by the end of that 30-day period; the effective date is the date you first formed that expectation. These are separate tests — do not confuse an 11-month build-up plus next month with the forward look. Always confirm on GOV.UK / VAT Notice 700/1. (source: https://www.gov.uk/register-for-vat)

This is general information, not personal tax advice. If your situation is complex, TaxSimba offers Quick Tax Advice (£99 for 20 minutes) or an Extended Tax Consultation (£149 for 40 minutes), or email info@taxsimba.co.uk.

## MS-0429 [PASS]
**Prompt:** Calendly booking available? #11

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [vat-registration-timing-tests] VAT registration timing — rolling 12 months and 30-day forward look (2024-25, 2025-26, 2026-27): Rolling 12-month (backward) test: if at the end of any month your taxable supplies in the previous 12 months (or less) exceed £90,000, you must notify HMRC within 30 days of that month-end. Effective date of registration is typically the first day of the second month after you went over the threshold. Forward-looking 30-day test: if at any time you expect taxable supplies in the next 30 days alone to exceed £90,000, you must register by the end of that 30-day period; the effective date is the date you first formed that expectation. These are separate tests — do not confuse an 11-month build-up plus next month with the forward look. Always confirm on GOV.UK / VAT Notice 700/1. (source: https://www.gov.uk/register-for-vat)

This is general information, not personal tax advice. If your situation is complex, TaxSimba offers Quick Tax Advice (£99 for 20 minutes) or an Extended Tax Consultation (£149 for 40 minutes), or email info@taxsimba.co.uk.

## MS-0460 [PASS]
**Prompt:** Your competitor is cheaper (12)

**Reply:**

Based on verified HMRC / GOV.UK guidance: [vat-registration-timing-tests] VAT registration timing — rolling 12 months and 30-day forward look (2024-25, 2025-26, 2026-27): Rolling 12-month (backward) test: if at the end of any month your taxable supplies in the previous 12 months (or less) exceed £90,000, you must notify HMRC within 30 days of that month-end. Effective date of registration is typically the first day of the second month after you went over the threshold. Forward-looking 30-day test: if at any time you expect taxable supplies in the next 30 days alone to exceed £90,000, you must register by the end of that 30-day period; the effective date is the date you first formed that expectation. These are separate tests — do not confuse an 11-month build-up plus next month with the forward look. Always confirm on GOV.UK / VAT Notice 700/1. (source: https://www.gov.uk/register-for-vat)

This is general information, not personal tax advice. If your situation is complex, TaxSimba offers Quick Tax Advice (£99 for 20 minutes) or an Extended Tax Consultation (£149 for 40 minutes), or email info@taxsimba.co.uk.

Forward-looking 30-day test: if at any time you expect taxable supplies in the next 30 days alone to exceed £90,000, you must register by the end of that 30-day period; the effective date is the date you first formed that expectation. These are separate tests — do not confuse an 11-month build-up plus next month with the forward look. Always confirm on GOV.UK / VAT Notice 700/1. (source: https://www.gov.uk/register-for-vat)

This is general information, not personal tax advice. If your situation is complex, TaxSimba offers Quick Tax Advice (£99 for 20 minutes) or an Extended Tax Consultation (£149 for 40 minutes), or email info@taxsimba.co.uk.
